Well, um... I guess it's been a little while now. Maybe around three months? 

I don't want to make it sound like it's been three months of just absolute misery, because it hasn't been like that at all. But I suppose that fogginess and just feeling a bit down... it's been most days since then. It started right around the time I began getting things in order to, um, prepare to leave the library. I'm sure the transition is just bringing up some normal feelings, right? I mean, anyone would feel a little out of sorts with a big change like that, wouldn't they? Oh, my weight? Well, um... I haven't really been stepping on the scale, so I'd hate to give you the wrong answer there. But... I suppose my appetite has been just a little off. Nothing too terrible, I promise! I definitely don't want you to think I'm starving myself or anything like that. 

It's just that, well, lately I sort of have to remind myself to sit down and eat. My wife actually noticed it before I did—she pointed out that I've been leaving my rice and soup half-finished. And usually, I would never waste a meal. I was always taught to clear my plate, so I feel a bit guilty about it, honestly. 

I know it's important to keep my strength up, especially at my age, so I really am trying to do better when she sets dinner out. I hope that's not too concerning? It's probably just a normal part of getting older and not burning as much energy, don't you think?

Oh, activities I used to enjoy? Well, let me think... I don't want to make it sound like I've just given up on everything, because I certainly haven't! I still try to keep busy. 

I suppose... well, I used to really enjoy tending to the little garden boxes we have out back. Just tomatoes and some herbs, nothing fancy. Lately, I haven't been out there quite as much. But I wouldn't say I find *no* pleasure in it at all. It's just that, well, the weather has been a bit unpredictable, and sometimes my knees bother me if I kneel too long. 

I still like the *idea* of gardening, if that makes sense? I just haven't had the same... I guess the same drive to get out there and pull the weeds recently. But I'm sure I'll get back to it once I'm fully retired and have more time on my hands. That's a pretty normal thing to happen when you're busy wrapping up a career, right? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around doing absolutely nothing all weekend.

Oh, goodness, no! Thoughts of... no, absolutely not. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that things are that bad. I would never, ever do something like that. I know how terrible that would be for my wife, and I'd never want to put her through that kind of pain.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hopeless? Oh, well... I wouldn't use a strong word like *hopeless*. I really don't want to sound like a pessimist, because I've always tried to look on the bright side of things. I'm sure things will perk up eventually. 

It's just... well, I suppose lately, most days I'll just sit at the kitchen table and... it's a bit silly to say, but it feels like the color has just gone out of everything. I used to pull out my old watercolor books to pass the time, but now looking at them just makes me feel sort of... heavy, I guess? Instead of interested. 

But I'm sure it's just a temporary rut, you know? Everyone gets into a funk sometimes, especially when retirement is looming and you're trying to figure out what's next. It doesn't mean I've given up hope entirely. It's just a phase, right? I'm sure with a little effort, I'll be back to my old self soon enough. I definitely don't want to be a bother or seem like I'm complaining too much!

Guilty? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I try really hard to do my best by everyone, you know? I've always believed in doing an honest day's work and being a good husband. 

I mean, I suppose sometimes I feel a little bad that I'm not... quite as energetic around the house as I used to be. More just... wishing I had a bit more pep in my step so my wife wouldn't have to pick up the slack. 

But she's very understanding, and I always make sure to thank her. I definitely don't have any dark, terrible secrets weighing on my conscience, if that's what you mean! I try to live a good life. That's the main thing, isn't it? Just trying to be a good person?

I don't want you to think I'm sitting around wringing my hands all day. \n\nI suppose everyone gets a little stressed now and then, especially with retiring soon, but... no, I don't think I've been feeling particularly anxious or worried. Just the normal amount, you know? I try to just take things one day at a time. I hope that's the right way to handle it? I certainly don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, my weight? Well, um... I haven't really been stepping on the scale, so I'd hate to give you the wrong answer there. But... I suppose my appetite has been just a little off. Nothing too terrible, I promise! I definitely don't want you to think I'm starving myself or anything like that. \n\nIt's just that, well, lately I sort of have to remind myself to sit down and eat. My wife actually noticed it before I did—she pointed out that I've been leaving my rice and soup half-finished. And usually, I would never waste a meal. I was always taught to clear my plate, so I feel a bit guilty about it, honestly. \n\nI know it's important to keep my strength up, especially at my age, so I really am trying to do better when she sets dinner out. I hope that's not too concerning? It's probably just a normal part of getting older and not burning as much energy, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find no pleasure in at all?\n\nPatient: Oh, activities I used to enjoy? Well, let me think... I don't want to make it sound like I've just given up on everything, because I certainly haven't! I still try to keep busy. \n\nI suppose... well, I used to really enjoy tending to the little garden boxes we have out back. Just tomatoes and some herbs, nothing fancy. Lately, I haven't been out there quite as much. But I wouldn't say I find *no* pleasure in it at all. It's just that, well, the weather has been a bit unpredictable, and sometimes my knees bother me if I kneel too long. \n\nI still like the *idea* of gardening, if that makes sense? I just haven't had the same... I guess the same drive to get out there and pull the weeds recently. But I'm sure I'll get back to it once I'm fully retired and have more time on my hands. That's a pretty normal thing to happen when you're busy wrapping up a career, right? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around doing absolutely nothing all weekend.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no! Thoughts of... no, absolutely not. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that things are that bad. I would never, ever do something like that. I know how terrible that would be for my wife, and I'd never want to put her through that kind of pain. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes when I'm feeling a bit low, I might think about how nice it would be to just... sleep for a very long time, you know? Just to get a break from feeling so tired. But that's just a passing thought, nothing serious at all. I think everyone has moments where they feel a little overwhelmed and just want a rest, right? \n\nI definitely don't want you to worry about me in that way. I'm perfectly safe, I promise. I just... I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to get back on track. That's why I'm here, talking to you. Well, I suppose... if I'm being completely honest, yes, it has been most days. I really do try to push through it, though! I don't just sit around feeling sorry for myself, I promise. 

I'd say this... this low mood and the fogginess have been hanging around for about three months now? It seemed to start right around the time I began getting my paperwork together to leave my job at the library. It's quite a big transition, retiring after all those years, you know? So I just figured feeling a little out of sorts was a perfectly natural reaction to such a major life change. 

I mean, anyone would feel a bit adrift when they're closing that chapter of their life, wouldn't they? So it's been a few months, but I'm sure it's just my mind trying to process the change. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way.

Oh, well, I wouldn't say it's interfering *significantly*... I really don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart! I'm still managing the basics, I promise. But... I suppose it has been a little more than mild, if I'm being completely open with you. 

It's actually quite embarrassing to admit, to be honest. I've always prided myself on being tidy and on top of things—working at the library, you really have to be, you know? But lately... well, I've let quite a bit pile up on my desk at home. My lecture plans for the local history talks I wanted to do, our travel notes for the retirement trip, and even just the simple household paperwork. 

I sit down to tackle it all, and I just... I can't seem to reliably organize myself the way I used to. The papers just kind of blur together and I end up walking away. But I'm sure once I get through this transition, I'll have it all sorted out in a weekend. It's perfectly normal to get a little behind when you're overwhelmed with a big life change, right? I just hope I don't sound too incompetent admitting all this to you.

Do you think that's normal for someone my age to feel sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose it's mostly just what I was saying a moment ago about the watercolors. I wouldn't say I've completely lost interest in absolutely everything, no. \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm just giving up and moping around the house all day—I know how lazy that sounds, and I do try to keep up with my regular routines. So, um, I haven't really noticed myself stopping other things, I don't think. I hope that's a good sign? Did I answer that okay for you?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's been a little while now. Maybe around three months? \n\nI don't want to make it sound like it's been three months of just absolute misery, because it hasn't been like that at all. But I suppose that fogginess and just feeling a bit down... it's been most days since then. It started right around the time I began getting things in order to, um, prepare to leave the library. I'm sure the transition is just bringing up some normal feelings, right? I mean, anyone would feel a little out of sorts with a big change like that, wouldn't they? I hope I'm not overreacting by bringing it up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, anxious? Well, no, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I'm a worrier or anything like that. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around wringing my hands all day. \n\nI suppose everyone gets a little stressed now and then, especially with retiring soon, but... no, I don't think I've been feeling particularly anxious or worried. Just the normal amount, you know? I try to just take things one day at a time. I hope that's the right way to handle it? I certainly don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, my weight? Well, um... I haven't really been stepping on the scale, so I'd hate to give you the wrong answer there. But... I suppose my appetite has been just a little off. Nothing too terrible, I promise! I definitely don't want you to think I'm starving myself or anything like that. \n\nIt's just that, well, lately I sort of have to remind myself to sit down and eat. My wife actually noticed it before I did—she pointed out that I've been leaving my rice and soup half-finished. And usually, I would never waste a meal. I was always taught to clear my plate, so I feel a bit guilty about it, honestly. \n\nI know it's important to keep my strength up, especially at my age, so I really am trying to do better when she sets dinner out. I hope that's not too concerning? It's probably just a normal part of getting older and not burning as much energy, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find no pleasure in at all?\n\nPatient: Oh, activities I used to enjoy? Well, let me think... I don't want to make it sound like I've just given up on everything, because I certainly haven't! I still try to keep busy. \n\nI suppose... well, I used to really enjoy tending to the little garden boxes we have out back. Just tomatoes and some herbs, nothing fancy. Lately, I haven't been out there quite as much. But I wouldn't say I find *no* pleasure in it at all. It's just that, well, the weather has been a bit unpredictable, and sometimes my knees bother me if I kneel too long. \n\nI still like the *idea* of gardening, if that makes sense? I just haven't had the same... I guess the same drive to get out there and pull the weeds recently. But I'm sure I'll get back to it once I'm fully retired and have more time on my hands. That's a pretty normal thing to happen when you're busy wrapping up a career, right? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around doing absolutely nothing all weekend.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no! Thoughts of... no, absolutely not. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that things are that bad. I would never, ever do something like that. I know how terrible that would be for my wife, and I'd never want to put her through that kind of pain. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes when I'm feeling a bit low, I might think about how nice it would be to just... sleep for a very long time, you know? Just to get a break from feeling so tired. But that's just a passing thought, nothing serious at all. I think everyone has moments where they feel a little overwhelmed and just want a rest, right? \n\nI definitely don't want you to worry about me in that way. I'm perfectly safe, I promise. I just... I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to get back on track. That's why I'm here, talking to you. That's the right attitude to have, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? Oh, well... I wouldn't use a strong word like *hopeless*. I really don't want to sound like a pessimist, because I've always tried to look on the bright side of things. I'm sure things will perk up eventually. \n\nIt's just... well, I suppose lately, most days I'll just sit at the kitchen table and... it's a bit silly to say, but it feels like the color has just gone out of everything. I used to pull out my old watercolor books to pass the time, but now looking at them just makes me feel sort of... heavy, I guess? Instead of interested. \n\nBut I'm sure it's just a temporary rut, you know? Everyone gets into a funk sometimes, especially when retirement is looming and you're trying to figure out what's next. It doesn't mean I've given up hope entirely. It's just a phase, right? I'm sure with a little effort, I'll be back to my old self soon enough. I definitely don't want to be a bother or seem like I'm complaining too much!\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I try really hard to do my best by everyone, you know? I've always believed in doing an honest day's work and being a good husband. \n\nI mean, I suppose sometimes I feel a little bad that I'm not... quite as energetic around the house as I used to be. Like with the gardening I mentioned, or sometimes I let the dishes sit in the sink a bit longer than I should. But I wouldn't call it guilt, exactly. More just... wishing I had a bit more pep in my step so my wife wouldn't have to pick up the slack. \n\nBut she's very understanding, and I always make sure to thank her. I definitely don't have any dark, terrible secrets weighing on my conscience, if that's what you mean! I try to live a good life. That's the main thing, isn't it? Just trying to be a good person?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of fogginess and lack of interest most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... if I'm being completely honest, yes, it has been most days. I really do try to push through it, though! I don't just sit around feeling sorry for myself, I promise. \n\nI'd say this... this low mood and the fogginess have been hanging around for about three months now? It seemed to start right around the time I began getting my paperwork together to leave my job at the library. It's quite a big transition, retiring after all those years, you know? So I just figured feeling a little out of sorts was a perfectly natural reaction to such a major life change. \n\nI mean, anyone would feel a bit adrift when they're closing that chapter of their life, wouldn't they? So it's been a few months, but I'm sure it's just my mind trying to process the change. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of fogginess and low mood are affecting your daily life in a mild way, or are they interfering more significantly with your ability to function?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I wouldn't say it's interfering *significantly*... I really don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart! I'm still managing the basics, I promise. But... I suppose it has been a little more than mild, if I'm being completely open with you. \n\nIt's actually quite embarrassing to admit, to be honest. I've always prided myself on being tidy and on top of things—working at the library, you really have to be, you know? But lately... well, I've let quite a bit pile up on my desk at home. My lecture plans for the local history talks I wanted to do, our travel notes for the retirement trip, and even just the simple household paperwork. \n\nI sit down to tackle it all, and I just... I can't seem to reliably organize myself the way I used to. The papers just kind of blur together and I end up walking away. But I'm sure once I get through this transition, I'll have it all sorted out in a weekend. It's perfectly normal to get a little behind when you're overwhelmed with a big life change, right? I just hope I don't sound too incompetent admitting all this to you.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. So I just figured feeling a little out of sorts was a perfectly natural reaction to such a major life change. \n\nI mean, anyone would feel a bit adrift when they're closing that chapter of their life, wouldn't they? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I... I suppose they kind of go hand in hand, if I'm being honest. I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around moping, because I do try to stay positive! But... well, most days lately, I find myself just sitting at the kitchen table, and it's like... like the color has just gone out of everything. 

And it's not just the paperwork I mentioned before. Even things I used to really enjoy... like my old watercolor books. I used to love flipping through them, getting ideas, you know? But now, I look at them and I just feel... heavy. Instead of interested, I just feel this weight. 

I'm sure it's just a phase, though. Everyone goes through little slumps where they don't feel quite like themselves, don't they? I really am trying to snap out of it. I just... I hope you don't think less of me for letting things get to me like this. It's just a bit harder to find the joy in things right now, but I'm sure I'll bounce back soon. That's the right attitude to have, isn't it?

I was always taught to clear my plate, so I feel a bit guilty about it, honestly. \n\nI know it's important to keep my strength up, especially at my age, so I really am trying to do better when she sets dinner out. I hope that's not too concerning? It's probably just a normal part of getting older and not burning as much energy, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find no pleasure in at all?\n\nPatient: Oh, activities I used to enjoy? I still try to keep busy. \n\nI suppose... well, I used to really enjoy tending to the little garden boxes we have out back. Just tomatoes and some herbs, nothing fancy. Lately, I haven't been out there quite as much. But I wouldn't say I find *no* pleasure in it at all. It's just that, well, the weather has been a bit unpredictable, and sometimes my knees bother me if I kneel too long. \n\nI still like the *idea* of gardening, if that makes sense? I just haven't had the same... I guess the same drive to get out there and pull the weeds recently. But I'm sure I'll get back to it once I'm fully retired and have more time on my hands. That's a pretty normal thing to happen when you're busy wrapping up a career, right? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around doing absolutely nothing all weekend.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no! Thoughts of... no, absolutely not. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that things are that bad. I would never, ever do something like that. I know how terrible that would be for my wife, and I'd never want to put her through that kind of pain. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes when I'm feeling a bit low, I might think about how nice it would be to just... sleep for a very long time, you know? Just to get a break from feeling so tired. But that's just a passing thought, nothing serious at all. I think everyone has moments where they feel a little overwhelmed and just want a rest, right? \n\nI definitely don't want you to worry about me in that way. I'm perfectly safe, I promise. I just... I just hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\n[Last Question]\nWould you say that your current state is making it difficult for you to carry out even the simplest daily tasks?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 60 / library_assistant_clerical / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Psychomotor_Changes\nmanifestation: \"My body feels slow, like even walking to the corner store or sorting papers on my desk takes more effort than it should.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I wouldn't say the *simplest* tasks... I mean, I'm still getting up and dressed, and I'm not completely helpless, I promise! I really don't want you to think I've just given up. But... I suppose things do feel a bit harder than they used to. 

It's actually a bit silly, but... my body just feels so slow lately. Like, even just walking down to the corner store, or... or like I said before, trying to sort through those papers on my desk. It just takes so much more effort than it should. It feels like I'm moving through molasses sometimes. 

But I'm sure that's just a normal part of getting a little older, right? At sixty, you can't expect to have the energy of a twenty-year-old! I'm sure it's nothing to worry too much about. I just need to push myself a little harder, wouldn't you agree? I really am trying my best not to let it slow me down too much.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today, I really appreciate you taking the time. \n\nI, um... I don't want to sound ungrateful, I have a good life. But, um... I try to pull out my old watercolor books, you know, to cheer myself up, but they just make me feel sort of... heavy, I guess, instead of interested. I'm sure it's just a phase, though. Do you think that's normal for someone my age to feel sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose it's mostly just what I was saying a moment ago about the watercolors. But I suppose that fogginess and just feeling a bit down... it's been most days since then. It started right around the time I began getting things in order to, um, prepare to leave the library. I'm sure the transition is just bringing up some normal feelings, right? I mean, anyone would feel a little out of sorts with a big change like that, wouldn't they? I hope I'm not overreacting by bringing it up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, anxious? Well, no, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I'm a worrier or anything like that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, my weight? Well, um... I haven't really been stepping on the scale, so I'd hate to give you the wrong answer there. But... I suppose my appetite has been just a little off. Nothing too terrible, I promise! I definitely don't want you to think I'm starving myself or anything like that. \n\nIt's just that, well, lately I sort of have to remind myself to sit down and eat. My wife actually noticed it before I did—she pointed out that I've been leaving my rice and soup half-finished. And usually, I would never waste a meal. I was always taught to clear my plate, so I feel a bit guilty about it, honestly. \n\nI know it's important to keep my strength up, especially at my age, so I really am trying to do better when she sets dinner out. I hope that's not too concerning? It's probably just a normal part of getting older and not burning as much energy, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find no pleasure in at all?\n\nPatient: Oh, activities I used to enjoy? Well, let me think... I don't want to make it sound like I've just given up on everything, because I certainly haven't! I still try to keep busy. \n\nI suppose... well, I used to really enjoy tending to the little garden boxes we have out back. Just tomatoes and some herbs, nothing fancy. Lately, I haven't been out there quite as much. But I wouldn't say I find *no* pleasure in it at all. It's just that, well, the weather has been a bit unpredictable, and sometimes my knees bother me if I kneel too long. \n\nI still like the *idea* of gardening, if that makes sense? I just haven't had the same... I guess the same drive to get out there and pull the weeds recently. But I'm sure I'll get back to it once I'm fully retired and have more time on my hands. That's a pretty normal thing to happen when you're busy wrapping up a career, right? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around doing absolutely nothing all weekend.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no! Thoughts of... no, absolutely not. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that things are that bad. I would never, ever do something like that. I know how terrible that would be for my wife, and I'd never want to put her through that kind of pain. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes when I'm feeling a bit low, I might think about how nice it would be to just... sleep for a very long time, you know? Just to get a break from feeling so tired. But that's just a passing thought, nothing serious at all. I think everyone has moments where they feel a little overwhelmed and just want a rest, right? \n\nI definitely don't want you to worry about me in that way. I'm perfectly safe, I promise. I just... I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to get back on track. That's why I'm here, talking to you. That's the right attitude to have, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? Oh, well... I wouldn't use a strong word like *hopeless*. I really don't want to sound like a pessimist, because I've always tried to look on the bright side of things. I'm sure things will perk up eventually. \n\nIt's just... well, I suppose lately, most days I'll just sit at the kitchen table and... it's a bit silly to say, but it feels like the color has just gone out of everything. I used to pull out my old watercolor books to pass the time, but now looking at them just makes me feel sort of... heavy, I guess? Instead of interested. \n\nBut I'm sure it's just a temporary rut, you know? Everyone gets into a funk sometimes, especially when retirement is looming and you're trying to figure out what's next. It doesn't mean I've given up hope entirely. It's just a phase, right? I'm sure with a little effort, I'll be back to my old self soon enough. I definitely don't want to be a bother or seem like I'm complaining too much!\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I try really hard to do my best by everyone, you know? I've always believed in doing an honest day's work and being a good husband. \n\nI mean, I suppose sometimes I feel a little bad that I'm not... quite as energetic around the house as I used to be. Like with the gardening I mentioned, or sometimes I let the dishes sit in the sink a bit longer than I should. But I wouldn't call it guilt, exactly. More just... wishing I had a bit more pep in my step so my wife wouldn't have to pick up the slack. \n\nBut she's very understanding, and I always make sure to thank her. I definitely don't have any dark, terrible secrets weighing on my conscience, if that's what you mean! I try to live a good life. That's the main thing, isn't it? Just trying to be a good person?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of fogginess and lack of interest most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... if I'm being completely honest, yes, it has been most days. I really do try to push through it, though! I don't just sit around feeling sorry for myself, I promise. \n\nI'd say this... this low mood and the fogginess have been hanging around for about three months now? It seemed to start right around the time I began getting my paperwork together to leave my job at the library. It's quite a big transition, retiring after all those years, you know? So I just figured feeling a little out of sorts was a perfectly natural reaction to such a major life change. \n\nI mean, anyone would feel a bit adrift when they're closing that chapter of their life, wouldn't they? So it's been a few months, but I'm sure it's just my mind trying to process the change. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of fogginess and low mood are affecting your daily life in a mild way, or are they interfering more significantly with your ability to function?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I wouldn't say it's interfering *significantly*... I really don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart! I'm still managing the basics, I promise. But... I suppose it has been a little more than mild, if I'm being completely open with you. \n\nIt's actually quite embarrassing to admit, to be honest. I've always prided myself on being tidy and on top of things—working at the library, you really have to be, you know? But lately... well, I've let quite a bit pile up on my desk at home. My lecture plans for the local history talks I wanted to do, our travel notes for the retirement trip, and even just the simple household paperwork. \n\nI sit down to tackle it all, and I just... I can't seem to reliably organize myself the way I used to. The papers just kind of blur together and I end up walking away. But I'm sure once I get through this transition, I'll have it all sorted out in a weekend. It's perfectly normal to get a little behind when you're overwhelmed with a big life change, right? I just hope I don't sound too incompetent admitting all this to you.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood and fogginess are affecting your daily life more than they are affecting your interest in activities?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose they kind of go hand in hand, if I'm being honest. I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around moping, because I do try to stay positive! But... well, most days lately, I find myself just sitting at the kitchen table, and it's like... like the color has just gone out of everything. \n\nAnd it's not just the paperwork I mentioned before. Even things I used to really enjoy... like my old watercolor books. I used to love flipping through them, getting ideas, you know? But now, I look at them and I just feel... heavy. Instead of interested, I just feel this weight. \n\nI'm sure it's just a phase, though. Everyone goes through little slumps where they don't feel quite like themselves, don't they? I really am trying to snap out of it. I just... I hope you don't think less of me for letting things get to me like this. It's just a bit harder to find the joy in things right now, but I'm sure I'll bounce back soon. That's the right attitude to have, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your current state is significantly impacting your ability to function in daily life?\n\nPatient: Well, I know I touched on this a moment ago, and I really don't want to sound like a broken record or—or like I'm incapable of taking care of myself. I'm really trying my best, I promise. But... yes, I suppose it is impacting things more than I'd like to admit. \n\nLike I mentioned about the paperwork... it's just so unlike me. I've let all my lecture plans for the history society, the notes for my retirement trip, and even just the regular household bills and things just... pile up on the desk. I sit down to do them, and I just can't seem to reliably organize myself the way I always used to. It's like my brain just won't sort the information properly right now. \n\nBut I'm sure it's just a temporary blip, right? Once I adjust to being retired, I'm sure my old organizational skills will come right back. It's perfectly understandable to drop the ball a little bit during a big transition... isn't it? I just hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your current state is making it difficult for you to carry out even the simplest daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say the *simplest* tasks... I suppose things do feel a bit harder than they used to. \n\nIt's actually a bit silly, but... my body just feels so slow lately. It feels like I'm moving through molasses sometimes. \n\nBut I'm sure that's just a normal part of getting a little older, right? At sixty, you can't expect to have the energy of a twenty-year-old! Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Would you say that your current state is significantly impacting your ability to function in daily life?\n\nPatient: Well, I know I touched on this a moment ago, and I really don't want to sound like a broken record or—or like I'm incapable of taking care of myself. I'm really trying my best, I promise. But... yes, I suppose it is impacting things more than I'd like to admit. \n\nLike I mentioned about the paperwork... it's just so unlike me. I've let all my lecture plans for the history society, the notes for my retirement trip, and even just the regular household bills and things just... pile up on the desk. I sit down to do them, and I just can't seem to reliably organize myself the way I always used to. It's like my brain just won't sort the information properly right now. \n\nBut I'm sure it's just a temporary blip, right? Once I adjust to being retired, I'm sure my old organizational skills will come right back. It's perfectly understandable to drop the ball a little bit during a big transition... isn't it? I just hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your current state is making it difficult for you to carry out even the simplest daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say the *simplest* tasks... I mean, I'm still getting up and dressed, and I'm not completely helpless, I promise! I really don't want you to think I've just given up. But... I suppose things do feel a bit harder than they used to. \n\nIt's actually a bit silly, but... my body just feels so slow lately. Like, even just walking down to the corner store, or... or like I said before, trying to sort through those papers on my desk. It just takes so much more effort than it should. I'm sure it's nothing to worry too much about. I just need to push myself a little harder, wouldn't you agree? I really am trying my best not to let it slow me down too much.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your current state is making it difficult for you to find pleasure in most activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, I wouldn't want you to think I just sit around moping from dawn till dusk! I really do try my hardest to put a brave face on and keep moving. Nobody likes a sourpuss, right? 

But... well, if I'm being completely honest with you... yes, I suppose it is. That low mood, and the fogginess in my head that I mentioned... they do tend to hang over me for a good portion of the day. Most days, actually. 

If I really think about it, it's been going on for about three months now. I first started noticing it right around the time I was preparing to leave my job at the library. You know, packing up my desk and getting ready to actually retire. 

I'm sure it's just the stress of such a big life change catching up with me, though. It's perfectly normal to feel a bit out of sorts and down for a few months during a major transition like that, don't you think? I just hope I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill by bringing it up.

I hope that's not too concerning? It's just that, well, the weather has been a bit unpredictable, and sometimes my knees bother me if I kneel too long. \n\nI still like the *idea* of gardening, if that makes sense? I just haven't had the same... I guess the same drive to get out there and pull the weeds recently. But I'm sure I'll get back to it once I'm fully retired and have more time on my hands. That's a pretty normal thing to happen when you're busy wrapping up a career, right? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around doing absolutely nothing all weekend.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no! Thoughts of... no, absolutely not. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that things are that bad. I would never, ever do something like that. I know how terrible that would be for my wife, and I'd never want to put her through that kind of pain. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes when I'm feeling a bit low, I might think about how nice it would be to just... sleep for a very long time, you know? Just to get a break from feeling so tired. But that's just a passing thought, nothing serious at all. I think everyone has moments where they feel a little overwhelmed and just want a rest, right? \n\nI definitely don't want you to worry about me in that way. I'm perfectly safe, I promise. I just... I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to get back on track. That's why I'm here, talking to you. It doesn't mean I've given up hope entirely. It's just a phase, right? I'm sure with a little effort, I'll be back to my old self soon enough. I definitely don't want to be a bother or seem like I'm complaining too much!\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I try really hard to do my best by everyone, you know? I've always believed in doing an honest day's work and being a good husband. \n\nI mean, I suppose sometimes I feel a little bad that I'm not... quite as energetic around the house as I used to be. Like with the gardening I mentioned, or sometimes I let the dishes sit in the sink a bit longer than I should. But I wouldn't call it guilt, exactly. More just... wishing I had a bit more pep in my step so my wife wouldn't have to pick up the slack. \n\nBut she's very understanding, and I always make sure to thank her. I definitely don't have any dark, terrible secrets weighing on my conscience, if that's what you mean! I try to live a good life. That's the main thing, isn't it? Just trying to be a good person?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of fogginess and lack of interest most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... if I'm being completely honest, yes, it has been most days. I don't just sit around feeling sorry for myself, I promise. \n\nI'd say this... this low mood and the fogginess have been hanging around for about three months now? So I just figured feeling a little out of sorts was a perfectly natural reaction to such a major life change. \n\nI mean, anyone would feel a bit adrift when they're closing that chapter of their life, wouldn't they? So it's been a few months, but I'm sure it's just my mind trying to process the change. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of fogginess and low mood are affecting your daily life in a mild way, or are they interfering more significantly with your ability to function?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I wouldn't say it's interfering *significantly*... I really don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart! I'm still managing the basics, I promise. But... I suppose it has been a little more than mild, if I'm being completely open with you. \n\nIt's actually quite embarrassing to admit, to be honest. I've always prided myself on being tidy and on top of things—working at the library, you really have to be, you know? But lately... well, I've let quite a bit pile up on my desk at home. I can't seem to reliably organize myself the way I used to. The papers just kind of blur together and I end up walking away. I'm sure things will perk up eventually. \n\nIt's just... well, I suppose lately, most days I'll just sit at the kitchen table and... it's a bit silly to say, but it feels like the color has just gone out of everything. I used to pull out my old watercolor books to pass the time, but now looking at them just makes me feel sort of... heavy, I guess? Instead of interested. \n\nBut I'm sure it's just a temporary rut, you know? Everyone gets into a funk sometimes, especially when retirement is looming and you're trying to figure out what's next. It doesn't mean I've given up hope entirely. It's just a phase, right? I'm sure with a little effort, I'll be back to my old self soon enough. I definitely don't want to be a bother or seem like I'm complaining too much!\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I try really hard to do my best by everyone, you know? I've always believed in doing an honest day's work and being a good husband. \n\nI mean, I suppose sometimes I feel a little bad that I'm not... quite as energetic around the house as I used to be. Like with the gardening I mentioned, or sometimes I let the dishes sit in the sink a bit longer than I should. But I wouldn't call it guilt, exactly. More just... wishing I had a bit more pep in my step so my wife wouldn't have to pick up the slack. \n\nBut she's very understanding, and I always make sure to thank her. I definitely don't have any dark, terrible secrets weighing on my conscience, if that's what you mean! I try to live a good life. That's the main thing, isn't it? Just trying to be a good person?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of fogginess and lack of interest most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... if I'm being completely honest, yes, it has been most days. I really do try to push through it, though! I don't just sit around feeling sorry for myself, I promise. \n\nI'd say this... this low mood and the fogginess have been hanging around for about three months now? It seemed to start right around the time I began getting my paperwork together to leave my job at the library. It's quite a big transition, retiring after all those years, you know? So I just figured feeling a little out of sorts was a perfectly natural reaction to such a major life change. \n\nI mean, anyone would feel a bit adrift when they're closing that chapter of their life, wouldn't they? I really don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart! I'm still managing the basics, I promise. But... I suppose it has been a little more than mild, if I'm being completely open with you. \n\nIt's actually quite embarrassing to admit, to be honest. I've always prided myself on being tidy and on top of things—working at the library, you really have to be, you know? But lately... well, I've let quite a bit pile up on my desk at home. My lecture plans for the local history talks I wanted to do, our travel notes for the retirement trip, and even just the simple household paperwork. \n\nI sit down to tackle it all, and I just... I can't seem to reliably organize myself the way I used to. The papers just kind of blur together and I end up walking away. But I'm sure once I get through this transition, I'll have it all sorted out in a weekend. It's perfectly normal to get a little behind when you're overwhelmed with a big life change, right? I just hope I don't sound too incompetent admitting all this to you.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood and fogginess are affecting your daily life more than they are affecting your interest in activities?\n\nPatient: Well, I... But... well, most days lately, I find myself just sitting at the kitchen table, and it's like... like the color has just gone out of everything. \n\nAnd it's not just the paperwork I mentioned before. Even things I used to really enjoy... like my old watercolor books. I used to love flipping through them, getting ideas, you know? But now, I look at them and I just feel... heavy. I really am trying to snap out of it. I just... I hope you don't think less of me for letting things get to me like this. It's just a bit harder to find the joy in things right now, but I'm sure I'll bounce back soon. I'm really trying my best, I promise. But... yes, I suppose it is impacting things more than I'd like to admit. \n\nLike I mentioned about the paperwork... it's just so unlike me. I've let all my lecture plans for the history society, the notes for my retirement trip, and even just the regular household bills and things just... pile up on the desk. I sit down to do them, and I just can't seem to reliably organize myself the way I always used to. It's like my brain just won't sort the information properly right now. \n\nBut I'm sure it's just a temporary blip, right? Well, I know I'm probably repeating myself a bit, and I really hope I'm not being a bother by going over the same things again. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers!

I definitely don't want you to think I'm starving myself or anything like that. \n\nIt's just that, well, lately I sort of have to remind myself to sit down and eat. My wife actually noticed it before I did—she pointed out that I've been leaving my rice and soup half-finished. And usually, I would never waste a meal. I was always taught to clear my plate, so I feel a bit guilty about it, honestly. \n\nI know it's important to keep my strength up, especially at my age, so I really am trying to do better when she sets dinner out. I hope that's not too concerning? It's probably just a normal part of getting older and not burning as much energy, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find no pleasure in at all?\n\nPatient: Oh, activities I used to enjoy? Well, let me think... I don't want to make it sound like I've just given up on everything, because I certainly haven't! I still try to keep busy. \n\nI suppose... well, I used to really enjoy tending to the little garden boxes we have out back. Just tomatoes and some herbs, nothing fancy. Lately, I haven't been out there quite as much. But I wouldn't say I find *no* pleasure in it at all. It's just that, well, the weather has been a bit unpredictable, and sometimes my knees bother me if I kneel too long. \n\nI still like the *idea* of gardening, if that makes sense? I just haven't had the same... I guess the same drive to get out there and pull the weeds recently. But I'm sure I'll get back to it once I'm fully retired and have more time on my hands. That's a pretty normal thing to happen when you're busy wrapping up a career, right? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around doing absolutely nothing all weekend.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no! Thoughts of... no, absolutely not. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that things are that bad. I would never, ever do something like that. I know how terrible that would be for my wife, and I'd never want to put her through that kind of pain. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes when I'm feeling a bit low, I might think about how nice it would be to just... sleep for a very long time, you know? Just to get a break from feeling so tired. But that's just a passing thought, nothing serious at all. I think everyone has moments where they feel a little overwhelmed and just want a rest, right? \n\nI definitely don't want you to worry about me in that way. I'm perfectly safe, I promise. I just... I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to get back on track. That's why I'm here, talking to you. That's the right attitude to have, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? Oh, well... I wouldn't use a strong word like *hopeless*. I really don't want to sound like a pessimist, because I've always tried to look on the bright side of things. I'm sure things will perk up eventually. \n\nIt's just... well, I suppose lately, most days I'll just sit at the kitchen table and... it's a bit silly to say, but it feels like the color has just gone out of everything. I used to pull out my old watercolor books to pass the time, but now looking at them just makes me feel sort of... heavy, I guess? Instead of interested. \n\nBut I'm sure it's just a temporary rut, you know? Everyone gets into a funk sometimes, especially when retirement is looming and you're trying to figure out what's next. It doesn't mean I've given up hope entirely. It's just a phase, right? I'm sure with a little effort, I'll be back to my old self soon enough. I definitely don't want to be a bother or seem like I'm complaining too much!\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I try really hard to do my best by everyone, you know? I've always believed in doing an honest day's work and being a good husband. \n\nI mean, I suppose sometimes I feel a little bad that I'm not... quite as energetic around the house as I used to be. Like with the gardening I mentioned, or sometimes I let the dishes sit in the sink a bit longer than I should. But I wouldn't call it guilt, exactly. More just... wishing I had a bit more pep in my step so my wife wouldn't have to pick up the slack. \n\nBut she's very understanding, and I always make sure to thank her. I definitely don't have any dark, terrible secrets weighing on my conscience, if that's what you mean! I try to live a good life. That's the main thing, isn't it? Just trying to be a good person?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of fogginess and lack of interest most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... if I'm being completely honest, yes, it has been most days. I really do try to push through it, though! I don't just sit around feeling sorry for myself, I promise. \n\nI'd say this... this low mood and the fogginess have been hanging around for about three months now? It seemed to start right around the time I began getting my paperwork together to leave my job at the library. It's quite a big transition, retiring after all those years, you know? So I just figured feeling a little out of sorts was a perfectly natural reaction to such a major life change. \n\nI mean, anyone would feel a bit adrift when they're closing that chapter of their life, wouldn't they? So it's been a few months, but I'm sure it's just my mind trying to process the change. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of fogginess and low mood are affecting your daily life in a mild way, or are they interfering more significantly with your ability to function?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I wouldn't say it's interfering *significantly*... I really don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart! I'm still managing the basics, I promise. But... I suppose it has been a little more than mild, if I'm being completely open with you. \n\nIt's actually quite embarrassing to admit, to be honest. I've always prided myself on being tidy and on top of things—working at the library, you really have to be, you know? But lately... well, I've let quite a bit pile up on my desk at home. My lecture plans for the local history talks I wanted to do, our travel notes for the retirement trip, and even just the simple household paperwork. \n\nI sit down to tackle it all, and I just... I can't seem to reliably organize myself the way I used to. The papers just kind of blur together and I end up walking away. But I'm sure once I get through this transition, I'll have it all sorted out in a weekend. It's perfectly normal to get a little behind when you're overwhelmed with a big life change, right? I just hope I don't sound too incompetent admitting all this to you.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood and fogginess are affecting your daily life more than they are affecting your interest in activities?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose they kind of go hand in hand, if I'm being honest. I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around moping, because I do try to stay positive! But... well, most days lately, I find myself just sitting at the kitchen table, and it's like... like the color has just gone out of everything. \n\nAnd it's not just the paperwork I mentioned before. Even things I used to really enjoy... like my old watercolor books. I used to love flipping through them, getting ideas, you know? But now, I look at them and I just feel... heavy. Instead of interested, I just feel this weight. \n\nI'm sure it's just a phase, though. Everyone goes through little slumps where they don't feel quite like themselves, don't they? I really am trying to snap out of it. I just... I hope you don't think less of me for letting things get to me like this. It's just a bit harder to find the joy in things right now, but I'm sure I'll bounce back soon. That's the right attitude to have, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your current state is significantly impacting your ability to function in daily life?\n\nPatient: Well, I know I touched on this a moment ago, and I really don't want to sound like a broken record or—or like I'm incapable of taking care of myself. I'm really trying my best, I promise. But... yes, I suppose it is impacting things more than I'd like to admit. \n\nLike I mentioned about the paperwork... it's just so unlike me. I've let all my lecture plans for the history society, the notes for my retirement trip, and even just the regular household bills and things just... pile up on the desk. I sit down to do them, and I just can't seem to reliably organize myself the way I always used to. It's like my brain just won't sort the information properly right now. \n\nBut I'm sure it's just a temporary blip, right? Once I adjust to being retired, I'm sure my old organizational skills will come right back. It's perfectly understandable to drop the ball a little bit during a big transition... isn't it? I just hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your current state is making it difficult for you to carry out even the simplest daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say the *simplest* tasks... I mean, I'm still getting up and dressed, and I'm not completely helpless, I promise! I really don't want you to think I've just given up. But... I suppose things do feel a bit harder than they used to. \n\nIt's actually a bit silly, but... my body just feels so slow lately. Like, even just walking down to the corner store, or... or like I said before, trying to sort through those papers on my desk. It just takes so much more effort than it should. It feels like I'm moving through molasses sometimes. \n\nBut I'm sure that's just a normal part of getting a little older, right? At sixty, you can't expect to have the energy of a twenty-year-old! I'm sure it's nothing to worry too much about. I just need to push myself a little harder, wouldn't you agree? I really am trying my best not to let it slow me down too much.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your current state is making it difficult for you to find pleasure in most activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I know I touched on this a little bit earlier, and I really hope I'm not talking your ear off or sounding like a complainer. I always try to look on the bright side! But... yes, I suppose that's exactly it. \n\nLike I was saying before about just sitting at the kitchen table... it really does feel like someone just washed all the color out of my days. And it's not for lack of trying, I promise! I still get out my old watercolor books, hoping they'll spark something, but... well, instead of feeling interested or inspired like I used to, I just get that heavy feeling in my chest. \n\nI'm still making an effort to find the good in things, though. It's just a bit of a rut, isn't it? I mean, it's perfectly normal to lose your enthusiasm for your hobbies every now and then... right? I just hope you don't think I'm not trying hard enough to stay positive.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood is present for more than half of the day?\n\nPatient: Oh, I wouldn't want you to think I just sit around moping from dawn till dusk! I really do try my hardest to put a brave face on and keep moving. Nobody likes a sourpuss, right? \n\nBut... well, if I'm being completely honest with you... yes, I suppose it is. That low mood, and the fogginess in my head that I mentioned... they do tend to hang over me for a good portion of the day. Most days, actually. \n\nIf I really think about it, it's been going on for about three months now. I first started noticing it right around the time I was preparing to leave my job at the library. You know, packing up my desk and getting ready to actually retire. \n\nI'm sure it's just the stress of such a big life change catching up with me, though. It's perfectly normal to feel a bit out of sorts and down for a few months during a major transition like that, don't you think? I just hope I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill by bringing it up.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood is interfering with your ability to enjoy things you used to like almost every day?\n\nPatient: Well, I know I'm probably repeating myself a bit, and I really hope I'm not being a bother by going over the same things again. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers! \n\nBut... yes, I suppose it is almost every day. Like I was saying, I'll just be sitting there at the kitchen table, and it's like... well, like all the color has just drained out of everything. And I really do try to push past it, I promise! I get out my watercolors, hoping I'll feel that same spark I used to get, but... instead of feeling interested, I just get this awful, heavy feeling. \n\nIt's silly to let it get to me so much, isn't it? I'm sure everyone goes through phases where their hobbies don't feel as fun for a little while. I just hope you don't think I'm being too negative about it all. I really am trying to keep my chin up!\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Oh, well... I really don't want to sound like I'm falling apart at the seams, you know? I try so hard to keep things running smoothly, and I'd hate for you to think I'm just letting everything go to ruin. 

But... if I'm being perfectly honest... it has been a bit of a struggle lately. I used to be so on top of things, especially with my background at the library. But now... well, I've sort of let things pile up a bit. My plans for those history lectures I wanted to attend, my travel notes for that trip I was hoping to take, even just the regular household paperwork... it's all just sitting there in stacks.

I mean, it's just a little clutter, right? Everyone gets behind on their filing sometimes... don't they? I just hope you don't think I'm being terribly lazy. I really am trying to get back on track!
